Like, at the clinic, if someone misplaced a medication cup or if there was a slight change in our morning schedule, I would just lose my temper with them. It really didn't feel like my usual self at all.\n\nDoctor: How long did that unusually upbeat or irritable period last?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure how long it lasted. I didn't really track the days. It was just a stretch of time earlier this month where I felt that way, but I couldn't give you an exact number of days.\n\nDoctor: During that unusually upbeat or irritable period, did you need less sleep than usual while still feeling rested?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. During that high-energy stretch, I was sleeping maybe three hours a night. I'd still get up before sunrise feeling completely sharp. I was just convinced I didn't really need any more rest than that.\n\nDoctor: About how many days did that unusually upbeat or irritable period last?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago, I really don't have an exact number for you. I wasn't keeping track of the days on a calendar or anything while it was happening. It was just a stretch of time earlier this month. I'm sorry, I just don't know exactly how long it was.\n\nDoctor: Did that unusually upbeat or irritable period last at least four days in a row?\n\nPatient: I... I really couldn't say for sure if it was four days in a row. I know you're looking for a specific timeframe, but like I was saying, I just wasn't paying attention to the calendar. It could have been four days, or maybe more, but... I honestly just don't know for certain.\n\nDoctor: How much did that unusually energized or irritable period interfere with your ability to function at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it interfered a lot. I’ve actually started making mistakes at the clinic, which really isn't like me. And it spills over into everything else, too. I’m avoiding my friends right now because... well, I’m afraid I’ll either snap at someone or just completely fall apart. \n\nIt's affecting my whole life, honestly. At home, my husband has had to take over basically everything. He's doing the bills, making the meals... and he's having to keep an eye on me to keep me safe when the thoughts get dark. So, yes, it's been very hard to function.\n\nDoctor: During that unusually energized period, did your thoughts feel unusually fast or difficult to keep up with?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. My mind wasn't really racing or... hard to keep up with, no. I mean, I had all that energy and irritability, but my thoughts themselves were moving at a pretty normal speed, I think. So, no, I didn't experience that.\n\nDoctor: During that energized or irritable period, did you become noticeably more active or take on more projects than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I did. I've never had to go to the hospital for... for being energized or irritable like that. Like I mentioned, my husband was watching me pretty closely at home, but it never got to the point where I needed to be admitted. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Was there any stretch of at least seven days in a row when you felt unusually energized or irritable like that?\n\nPatient: No, it wasn't a full week.
